## Document 117: 9月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

Date: 2026-10-15

证券研究报告

9 月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
外币贷款同比多增。9 月新增外币贷款 349 亿元，同比多增 478 亿元，可
能与当月进口增速偏强，企业进口付汇需求增加有关。委托贷款同比多
增。9 月新增委托贷款 802 亿元，同比多增 519 亿元。一方面，可能与新
型政策性金融工具开始投放有关。部分资金通过股东借款的形式投入项
目。相关基金委托政策性银行向项目公司股东提供借款，带动委托贷款增
加；另一方面，公积金贷款需求边际改善亦对委托贷款形成一定支撑。未
贴现银行承兑汇票同比少增。9 月未贴现银行承兑汇票增加 2153 亿元，
同比少增 1081 亿元，主要与季末银行加大收票力度，票据被贴现至表内
有关。 
M1 增速回落。9 月 M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月回落 0.9 个百分点。或主
要受 9 月资本市场调整、市场风险偏好边际回落影响，资金活化程度有所
减弱。9 月非银存款亦减少 1.9 万亿元，同比多减 8500 亿元。 
M2 增速小幅回落。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月回落 0.3 个百分点。
一是信贷派生仍然偏弱，新增人民币贷款同比少增，银行体系通过信贷 派
生存款的能力受限；二是财政存款同比少减 4100 亿元，财政资金向企业
和居民存款转化力度偏弱，亦对 M2 形成一定拖累。 
政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳 。往前看，一是货币端，PSL 利率下调
至 1.5%，支持范围扩展至“六张网”，叠加科技创新和技术改造再贷
款、支农支小再贷款额度增加，有助于降低重点领域融资成本、拓宽重大
项目和薄弱环节的融资渠道，为后续信贷投放提供支撑 ；二是准财政端，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已启动投放，项目资本金补充有望撬动配
套贷款及后续债券融资；三是财政端，5500 亿元地方政府债务结存限额
已安排使用，其中 2500 亿元专项债重点支持在建项目和“六张网”等领
域。叠加四季度政府债发行提速，相关政策效果有望逐步显现，对社融企
稳形成有效支撑。

风险提示：宏观经济不及预期，地产销售不及预期，美国货币政策超预
期，地缘政治风险超预期。

## Document 205: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

At current levels, the US 10-year government bond yield 
offers investors compelling risk-reward trade off

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations 
Our policy rate outlook for the Fed, ECB and BoJ

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 236: First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #newzealand
First Impressions: NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion,
September quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
08:35 October 06 2026 Share
Businesses are feeling more optimistic about the months ahead, but current conditions
remain tough for many industries. Cost pressures eased off a little compared to last quarter.
Key results (seasonally adjusted)
General business situation: +40 (Prev: +14)

### Page 2

Trading activity, past three months: -1 (Prev: +1) 
Trading activity, next three months: +15 (Prev: +11) 
Average selling prices, past three months: +27 (Prev: +37) 
Average selling prices, next three months: +45 (Prev: +51)

The NZIER’s quarterly survey of business opinion showed a strong lift in general confidence in
September, despite resurgent fuel prices and the uncertainty around the looming election. Firms were
more confident about their prospects for the next few months, even though their recent performance
remained rough in many industries. 
The survey was held over a relatively compressed period (9 to 28 September), with around 40% of
responses coming back on the first day. Later responses were less confident, as the resurgence in
world oil prices was finally reflected in prices at the pump over the course of the month. 
General business sentiment rose to a net 40% positive compared to a net 14% in June, returning to
around the levels seen in the December quarter last year (before the Middle East conflict). However,
the own-activity measures, which tend to have a closer correspondence with GDP growth, were more
subdued. Trading activity over the last three months was slightly weaker than in June, while
expectations for the quarter ahead saw a more modest four-point increase to +15%. 
Other forward-looking measures were also up for the quarter. Hiring intentions rose strongly to a net
23% positive (although past employment was down). Notably, firms continue to report that workers are
becoming harder to find, despite the ongoing rise in unemployment over the last couple of years. That
said, the proportion of firms citing labour as a factor limiting output declined this quarter. Investment
intentions picked up, more so for plant and machinery than for buildings. 
The results were mixed across industries. Manufacturing and services were more upbeat for the
months ahead but reported substantially weaker performance over the last quarter. In contrast, builders
and merchants reported both stronger past results and greater optimism about the near-term outlook. 
The pricing measures in the survey were down compared to June – not entirely surprising as fuel
prices were lower on average through the September quarter compared to the June quarter. The
notable exception was merchants, who expected both higher costs and prices for the quarter ahead. 
The implications for the RBNZ ahead of its 28 October policy review are unclear – there is certainly no
smoking gun in this report that would demand that the RBNZ abandon the “pause” strategy
communicated at the September MPS. Optimism among businesses remains high despite the obvious
headwinds, but there is still a wide gap between hopes and reality. The pricing measures have eased
compared to last quarter, but that may simply confirm the RBNZ’s expectation that annual inflation will
drop from the 4.1% recorded last quarter as fuel prices eased. The September quarter CPI, released
on 22 October, may be more informative in that respect. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

registration number HRB 118483.  In accordance with APRA’s Prudential Standard 222 ‘Association
with Related Entities’, Westpac does not stand behind WEG other than as provided for in certain

## Document 240: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

服務與能源同步⾛⾼時，應降低⾼估值成⻑股與⻑天期公債部位，保留短券與現⾦。

核⼼ PCE 三個⽉年化維持約 2% 且 10 年期殖利率停⽌創⾼時，才逐步把到期短券轉向⾼品質股

價⼒最強的環節，但⾼ Beta 個股波動加⼤，應以⿓頭為核⼼、⾼ Beta 為衛星並分批調整。
2   當 DRAM 與 NAND 平均售價先於需求下滑，或⻑約出現減量與延期時，應快速下修記憶體部
位；在報價與位元出貨仍同向成⻑前，股價回檔屬估值重置⽽⾮需求轉折。
3   台積電 ADR 連五⽇下跌，顯⽰資⾦正重新排序⾼估值 AI 硬體；本週台積電與 ASML 第三季財報
是驗證點，當營收、⽑利率與資本⽀出指引同步上修時，才宜回補先進製程與封裝部位。

### Page 4

1   美元指數 10/12 上漲 0.4% 收在 102.58 ，盤中⾼點 102.61 ，為 2025 年 4 ⽉以來最⾼；相對過去 52

點，⽇圓在每美元 158.5 附近，逼近曾觸發⽇本當局⼲預的 160 關⼝。
3   推升美元的因素包括中東衝突推⾼油價、市場預期美國通膨再升溫，以及聯準會官員偏鷹發

4   哈薩克央⾏副⾏⻑表⽰，美債殖利率處於多年⾼位，使美元⽐⿈⾦更具吸引⼒，該⾏將把⿈⾦
占外匯儲備⽐重降低約 3 個百分點，反映部分央⾏購⾦需求轉弱。
5   ⿈⾦ 10/12 下跌約 0.6% ⾄每盎司約 4,166 美元，較 52 週⾼點 5,586 美元回落約 25% （推算）；本
週美國 9 ⽉ CPI 若⾼於預期，美元與殖利率可能再⾛⾼。

三⼤結論
1   美元強勢來⾃利差與避險，但⻑線仍受美國逾 40 兆美元債務與⻑債供給牽制，不宜視為單向
交易；保留美元短券作為流動性部位，並依實質利差調節避險⽐率。
2   美元升值提⾼台幣投資⼈的海外資產換匯報酬，也壓抑亞幣與外資流⼊；當美元指數續升且台
韓外資持續流出時，應保留美元現⾦與部分避險，降低未避險的集中曝險。
3   ⿈⾦受美元與實質利率壓制，但財政與地緣尾端⾵險仍在；⿈⾦維持 10% ⾄ 15% 分批避險配
置，當實質利率快速上升時暫緩追價，⽽⾮因短線回落全⾯撤出。

歐股維持衛星配置，以低負債、⾼現⾦流與國防基建受益者為主。

加且⾃由現⾦流未因資本⽀出惡化時，可提⾼雲端與軟體部位，否則維持中性。
3   ⾼利率下股價反應分化，平台公司上漲、上游硬體回檔；配置上維持台積電、先進封裝與液冷
等瓶頸環節為核⼼，對只依賴資本⽀出敘事的標的降低權重。

並取消電⼦菸裝置等產品的增值稅出⼝退稅。
2   上述產品中徵收消費稅者，出⼝消費稅政策不變、續⽤退（免）稅；適⽤稅率以出⼝報關單註

明的出⼝⽇期認定，⾞廠約有五個多⽉緩衝期。
3   退稅率下降 4 個百分點，等同出⼝⾞每單位貨值多承擔約 4% 的增值稅成本（推算），將壓縮以
價格競爭打⼊海外市場的⾞廠⽑利；今年 1 ⽉中國已公告取消光伏產品出⼝退稅。
4   中汽協 10/12 公布 9 ⽉新能源⾞銷量 182.3 萬輛、年增 13.6% ，占新⾞銷量 61.6% ，連續三個⽉⾼
於 60% ；國內滲透率已⾼，出⼝是⾞廠消化產能的重要管道。
5   新稅率 2027 年 4 ⽉才⽣效，⽣效前可能出現提前出⼝，使 2027 年第⼀季出⼝基期墊⾼，後續解
讀中國汽⾞出⼝數據時需留意這項扭曲。
三⼤結論
1   退稅下調壓縮出⼝⾞廠的價格空間，對依賴出⼝消化產能的中國⾞廠屬利空；中國股市維持低
權重、選擇性配置，不因單⼀產業政策調整改變整體判斷。
2   政策⽅向是減少低價出⼝補貼，短期有助緩和貿易摩擦，但中美休戰只延⾄ 2027 年 1 ⽉ 10 ⽇；
當關稅、稀⼟與 H200 晶⽚交貨出現履約變數時，應進⼀步降低中國出⼝導向部位。

3   中國 8 ⽉社會消費品零售僅年增 0.4% ，⾞市依賴補貼與出⼝；當社會零售、⺠間投資與房地產
資⾦同步回升時，才考慮提⾼中國消費相關部位，在此之前只保留⾼技術製造題材。

### Page 7

事件  8
10/12 外資賣超台股 522 億元、連四⽇賣超，加權指數收平（台灣時間  10/12
13:30 ）
五⼤重點
1   外資 10/12 賣超上市股票 522 億元，連續第四個交易⽇賣超，近 5 ⽇累計賣超 758.6 億元、近 20
⽇ 1,623.2 億元；投信買超 71.2 億元，⾃營商賣超 142.9 億元。
2   加權指數收 49,301.96 點，下跌 11.48 點、跌幅 0.02% ，盤中低點 48,768.82 點；櫃買指數漲
0.28% ⾄ 427.90 點，上漲 1,130 家多於下跌 1,034 家。
3   台積電是外資賣超主軸， 10/12 外資賣超 9,422 張，股價收 2,540 元、跌 0.39% ；連同 10/8 賣超
12,302 張，兩個交易⽇合計逾 2.1 萬張（推算）。
4   盤中由⾮電⼦族群撐盤，⻑榮獲外資買超約 1.2 萬張並漲停，顯⽰外資調節集中在權值電⼦
股，並⾮全⾯撤出台股。
5   融資餘額 10/8 為 1,182.6 萬張，位居近⼀年 93 百分位，近 20 ⽇增加 47.1 萬張；市值加權本益⽐

26.5 倍、預估本益⽐ 22.3 倍，籌碼與評價都偏緊。
三⼤結論
1   台灣出⼝、 PMI （採購經理⼈指數）與外銷訂單仍⽀持擴張，外資賣超屬⾼檔調節⽽⾮基本⾯
轉弱；台股維持核⼼配置，不追單⽇突破，採拉回分批，並以內需與⾦融股降低半導體集中度。
2   融資餘額處近⼀年⾼位、外資連賣，若台積電本週財報不如預期，散戶槓桿可能放⼤回檔；當
外資現貨與期貨同步偏空且美元續強時，應先降低槓桿與⾼ Beta 部位。
3   外資匯出與美元⾛強同時出現，新台幣短線偏弱；持有台股者適度分散幣別，搭配美元短券部
分對沖，不以單⼀匯率點位作為進出依據。
事件  9
10/11 川普宣布俄烏「能源停⽕」，⽩宮稱仍屬潛在協議（台灣時間  10/11
22:17 ）
五⼤重點
1   美國總統川普 10/11 宣布俄烏戰爭「能源停⽕」即刻⽣效，稱雙⽅同意不再破壞彼此能源設
施；⽩宮官員隨後說明，這是指雙⽅停⽌攻擊煉油廠的潛在協議，後續可能延伸⾄電廠與電網。

### Page 8

2   ⽩宮官員也承認⽅案不對稱：烏克蘭只有⼀座屢遭攻擊、未完全運轉的煉油廠，俄羅斯則擁有
規模⼤得多的煉油網路。
3   烏克蘭總統澤倫斯基表⽰對川普的說法感到意外，但若俄⽅同意，烏⽅願停⽌攻擊俄國煉油
廠；他 10/12 再指控俄⽅持續攻擊烏克蘭能源設施，基輔當晚發⽣爆炸。
4   消息⼀度壓低油價， 10/12 亞洲早盤 WTI 跌 2.00% ⾄每桶 89.14 美元、布蘭特跌 1.9% ⾄ 100.72 美
元；但中東攻擊隨後升溫，布蘭特當⽇反⽽收漲 1.84% 。
5   俄羅斯副總理諾⽡克 10/12 表⽰，俄國企業正與買家洽談柴油銷售，呼應川普 10/10 所稱俄⽅將
供應⼤量柴油；若落實，可緩解全球柴油供給緊張。
三⼤結論
1   能源停⽕仍停留在宣⽰階段，雙⽅攻擊未停，油價重新由中東供給⾵險主導；原油維持事件避
險，不因停⽕消息提前⼤幅降低部位。
2   若俄烏能源停⽕落實、俄國柴油出⼝恢復，成品油價可望回落，有助通膨在⾼基期後下降；當
運輸恢復且庫存回補時，應降低原油部位。

3   歐洲是能源停⽕最直接的受益者，但法國財政與能源成本仍壓抑估值；當天然氣與柴油價格同
步回落、歐元區通膨連續下降時，才宜提⾼歐洲週期股⽐重。
事件  10
10/10 中國發改委：加⼤逆週期調節，推動重⼤⼯程儘早開⼯（台灣時間  10/10
19:02 ）
五⼤重點
1   中國國家發改委主任鄭柵潔 10/10 主持⺠營企業座談會表⽰，第四季是鞏固經濟回升的關鍵窗
⼝，將加⼤逆週期調節⼒度，強化存量與增量政策協同。
2   他表⽰將推動「⼗五五」規劃與「六張網」建設實施⽅案中明確的重⼤⼯程儘早開⼯，以釋放
內需潛⼒，並推動科技創新與產業創新深度融合。
3   座談企業涵蓋⽯化、天然氣供應、農業機械與 AI 應⽤，議題聚焦第三季經濟形勢與國際供需變
化，反映能源成本與外需波動是當前政策關注重點。
4   消息帶動 10/12 A 股電網設備族群開⾼、⾦冠電氣漲停，但上證指數當天收跌 0.43% 、創業板指
跌 2.61% ，逾 4,300 檔個股下跌，政策利多未扭轉整體賣壓。

### Page 9

5   國家統計局數據顯⽰， 8 ⽉社會消費品零售年增僅 0.4% ， 1 ⾄ 8 ⽉固定資產投資年減 7.2% ，其中
房地產開發投資年減 19.9% ，內需仍是政策要補的缺⼝。
三⼤結論
1   發改委強調逆週期與重⼤⼯程，屬穩投資⽅向，可⽀撐電網與基建相關族群；但⺠間投資與房
地產仍負成⻑，政策效果偏供給側，中國股市維持低權重、選擇性配置。
2   重⼤⼯程若加速開⼯，對銅與電⼒設備需求有⽀撐，並與全球電網升級趨勢相互加成；當現貨
與庫存結構確認供給缺⼝時，可分批持有銅，避免追逐單次政策⾏情。
3   當 9 ⽉社會零售、⺠間投資與房地產資⾦同步改善時，才考慮提⾼中國內需部位；在此之前，
中國資產僅保留⾼技術製造與政策受惠題材。
待確認
1. 推算值：事件 4 美元指數⾃ 52 週低點回升約 7.4% 、⿈⾦較 52 週⾼點回落約 25% ；事件 7 退稅成本約 4% ；事件 8 台積
電兩⽇外資賣超合計逾 2.1 萬張，均為依公開數字⾃⾏推算。
2. 事件 1 ：克⾥夫蘭聯儲官網截⾄本報告未刊出 10/12 講稿，內容以專訪與公開發⾔的即時紀錄為準；美國 9 ⽉ CPI 與

沃什發⾔的確切⽇期待官⽅⾏事曆確認。
3. 事件 2 ：胡塞武裝對機場與阿美設施的攻擊細節為其單⽅宣稱，沙烏地官⽅未逐項證實；超⼤型油輪停航數量為船
舶追蹤資料，屬單⼀來源。
4. 事件 3 ：⻑鑫存儲技術進展被視為賣壓主因，屬市場解讀；閃迪、 Lumentum 、 Semtech 跌幅為收盤報導數字，未
逐⼀以交易所資料核對。

屬不同主題。

## Document 250: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%)

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

IEA 成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺优先 “ 尽快 ” 释放

摘要：
国际能源署（ IEA ）成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺 " 尽
快 " 释放其中 2000 万桶柴油。然⽽，这批储备本质上是对既有承诺的加速兑现，⽽⾮新⼀轮⼲预
⾏动。
据彭博报道， IEA 理事会于当地时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇周四召开会议，会后发表声明称，成员国将加快分
配剩余储量，包括 " 尽快 " 释放 2000 万桶柴油。 IEA 同时指出，由于部分成员国释放量超出初始承
诺，最终实际释放总量将超过 3 ⽉宣布的 4 亿桶上限。
油价对此反应有限。 WTI 原油本周累计上涨约 6% ，周四单⽇涨幅达 3% ，⽬前交投于每桶 98 美元

下⽅；布伦特原油则稳定在每桶 106 美元附近。霍尔⽊兹海峡局势持续紧张，仍是市场主要上⾏
⻛险来源。
1 亿桶是旧账
IEA 于 2026 年 3 ⽉宣布释放 4 亿桶⽯油储备，以应对伊朗战争引发的供应中断。截⾄⽬前，成员
国已释放 3.3 亿桶，此次 1 亿桶系补⾜剩余缺⼝。
此前，摩根⼤通分析师明确指出，这批储备释放 " 在很⼤程度上是加速兑现此前已有承诺，⽽⾮
代表规模达 1 亿桶的全新⼲预⾏动 " 。
欧盟委员会发⾔⼈亦表态称，计划中的储备释放 " 必须在 3 ⽉批准的总量范围内推进 " 。德国经济
部确认将参与释放，但未披露任何新增规模。
美国施压，欧洲划定边界
此次会议背后，美欧之间存在明显分歧。

## Document 259: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Insurance payments include renters, auto or home, but do not include insurance escrowed into mortgage payments or deducted from a 
paycheck.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 275: Meta发布8160亿参数“美国最强开放权重模型”，联手苹果让大模型跑上Mac，最强模型仍闭源

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta发布8160亿参数“美国最强开放权重模型”，联手苹果让大模型跑上Mac，最强模型仍闭源.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Muse Spark Open 实际激活参数 420 亿，⽀持 100 万 Token 上下⽂窗⼝，主攻编程与智能体
任务，开发者可通过多个云平台获取，能⼒不及本周⼀推出的 Muse Wonder ；可在两台搭
载苹果 M5 Ultra 芯⽚的 Mac Studio 运⾏。扎克伯格发帖配图称该模型在新 Mac Studio 运⾏
出⾊。 Meta AI 负责⼈称，出于安全性和能⼒两⽅⾯考虑， Meta 通常不会开源最强⼤模
型，但将持续开源优秀模型；免费开放 Muse Spark Open 不会削弱 Meta 在筹建的云业务。
摘要：
Meta 推出开放权重模型 Muse Spark Open ，允许开发者下载模型权重、⾃⾏部署和定制，并与
苹果合作，让模型能够在两台 Mac Studio 上运⾏。与此同时， Meta 仍将能⼒更强的 Muse
Wonder 系列保持闭源，试图在开放开发者⽣态与 AI 商业化之间寻找平衡。
美东时间 15 ⽇周四， Meta 发布号称 “ 迄今美国能⼒最强的开放权重模型 ”Muse Spark Open 。该

模型总参数量达 8160 亿，⽀持 100 万 Token 上下⽂窗⼝，主攻编程与智能体任务，重点提升⻓周
期任务执⾏、复杂软件⼯程和多步推理能⼒。
Meta 当天公布 Muse Spark Open 的模型权重。开发者可以通过多个云平台获取模型，也可以下
载权重，在兼容的硬件和推理框架上⾃⾏运⾏，并使⽤ PyTorch 等⼯具进⼀步定制。模型采⽤
Muse Model License 授权，附带有限商业条款，允许许多开发者免费使⽤。
虽然 Muse Spark Open 的能⼒不及 Meta 本周⼀推出的 Muse Wonder ，但此次发布兑现了 Meta 此
前让其 AI 技术⼴泛向开发者开放的承诺。 Meta 曾凭借 Llama 系列推动开放模型⽣态，在上⼀轮模
型竞争中表现未达预期后，扎克伯格重组 AI 研发体系，转向打造能⼒更强的闭源模型。此次重新
开放模型权重意味着， Meta 正尝试在扩⼤开发者⽣态与保留前沿技术优势之间寻找新的平衡。
8160 亿参数、百万 Token 上下⽂，主攻编程与智能体

Meta 在官⽅博客中表⽰， Muse Spark Open 总参数量为 8160 亿，实际激活参数为 420 亿，拥有
100 万 Token 上下⽂窗⼝，并原⽣⽀持⽂本、图像、视频和⾳频。
与主要⽤于对话和内容⽣成的模型相⽐， Muse Spark Open 更强调⻓周期智能体⼯作。 Meta
称，该模型针对复杂软件⼯程、多步骤推理和需要持续推进的任务进⾏了优化，⽬标是让开发
者构建能够规划、调⽤⼯具并连续执⾏任务的 AI 智能体。
和官⽅博客⼀样， Meta AI 负责⼈、公司⾸席 AI 官 Alexandr Wang 在社交媒体上也将 Muse Spark
Open 称为迄今美国能⼒最强的开放权重模型。他表⽰，该模型专为编码和智能体⼯作设计，例
如⻓时程任务、复杂的软件⼯程、多步骤推理，并转发了 Meta 博客公布的以下该模型多项基准
指标测评成绩与竞品⼤模型对⽐。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

不过， “ 美国最强 ” 仍是 Meta 对⾃⾝产品的评价，并⾮已经获得全⾯独⽴验证的⾏业排名。开放权
重模型的竞争⼒不仅取决于参数规模，还取决于推理效果、运⾏成本、部署难度和开发者实际
使⽤体验。
此次开放的重点是模型权重，⽽⾮意味着所有训练数据、完整训练流程和研发细节均向外界公
开。开发者能够在授权范围内⾃⾏部署和调整模型，但其商业使⽤仍须遵守 Muse Model
License 的相关条款。
Meta 与苹果合作，双 Mac Studio 运⾏⼤模型
此次发布的⼀⼤亮点，是 Meta 与苹果合作，让 Muse Spark Open 能够借助最新 MLX 框架在两台
搭载 M5 Ultra 芯⽚的 Mac Studio 上运⾏。 Meta 官⽅博客称，这是⾸次通过搭载苹果 M5 Ultra 芯⽚
的 Mac Studio 为⽤⼾的桌⾯端带来领先的智能体功能。
扎克伯格在社交媒体发帖称， Muse Spark Open 在新 Mac Studio 上运⾏出⾊，感谢合作⽅苹果
公司及其 CEO 特努斯，并附上⼀张显⽰新模型在 Mac 电脑上运⾏的照⽚。

### Page 3

Meta 表⽰，苹果芯⽚的统⼀内存架构是实现本地部署的关键。每台 M5 Ultra Mac Studio 可提供
最⾼ 512GB 统⼀内存，两台设备协同后能够容纳整个模型。 Meta 称，两台机器可像⼀台拥有约
1TB 统⼀内存的系统⼀样协同⼯作。
媒体认为，这⼀合作为开发者提供了新的选择：他们可以在⾃⼰的硬件上运⾏先进模型，⽽不
必完全依赖云端服务器租⽤算⼒。对于软件开发、研究和视频制作等场景，本地部署也有助于
开发者更直接地控制模型运⾏环境。
Meta 在官⽅博客中还展⽰了模型的本地智能体⼯作流程，包括借助 Muse Code 修改开源 iOS 应
⽤、修复问题并完成构建，以及在 DaVinci Resolve 中逐帧分析视频素材、筛选精彩⽚段并调整
画⾯⽐例。
需要注意的是，模型能够在两台 Mac Studio 上运⾏，并不意味着普通 Mac 电脑也能以相同⽅式运
⾏这⼀完整模型。 Meta 此次展⽰针对的是⾼端硬件配置，本地部署仍⾯临设备成本、内存需求
和推理效率等限制。
对苹果⽽⾔，这⼀合作也提供了展⽰⾼端 Mac 计算能⼒的新场景；对 Meta ⽽⾔，则有助于证明

开放权重模型能够脱离⾃有云端基础设施，在开发者⾃⼰的设备上运⾏。两家公司⻓期以来在
隐私政策、⼴告追踪和计算平台等领域存在竞争，此次合作说明， AI 竞赛正在促使硅⾕企业重新
评估合作边界。
权重免费开放，多平台⽀持降低开发⻔槛
Meta 表⽰， Muse Spark Open 已通过 Baseten 、 Fireworks AI 、 Groq 、 Modal 、 Nebius 、
OpenRouter 和 Together AI 等平台提供 API 服务。模型权重则可从亚⻢逊 SageMaker AI 、⾕歌
Cloud Model Garden 、 Hugging Face 和微软 Foundry Models 等渠道获取。
在 推 理 框 架⽅ ⾯ ， 该 模 型⽀ 持 S G L a n g 、 v L L M 、 Te n s o r RT- L L M 、 H u g g i n g  F a c e
Transformers 、 Apple MLX 、 llama.cpp 和 Unsloth ，并针对 AMD 、苹果、英特尔和英伟达等硬件

平台进⾏了优化。

### Page 4

开发者还可以使⽤ PyTorch 及 TorchTitan 对模型进⾏进⼀步微调，以适应不同应⽤场景。 Meta
称，模型采⽤附带有限商业条款的 Muse Model License ，许多开发者可以免费使⽤。
这套发布⽅式的核⼼，是降低开发者试⽤、部署和定制模型的⻔槛。开发者可以先下载权重，
在本地或⾃⼰的基础设施上开展实验；当需求增⻓、需要更⼤规模的推理能⼒或额外⼯具时，
再选择云端 API 和算⼒服务。
但开放权重并不等于完全没有使⽤限制，也不意味着所有开发者都能零成本运⾏完整模型。硬
件采购、部署运维和推理算⼒仍可能带来可观成本，商业应⽤也需要遵循授权条款。
最强模型仍闭源， Meta 试图兼顾开放⽣态与商业化
Meta 周四透露， Muse Spark Open 的能⼒弱于该司本周⼀推出的 Muse Wonder 。 Meta 选择开放
较低⼀档模型的权重，同时将最先进模型继续保持闭源，反映出公司 AI 战略的变化。
Alexandr Wang 在接受彭博社采访时表⽰： “ 出于安全性和能⼒两⽅⾯的考虑，我们通常不会开

源我们最强⼤的模型。但我们确实认为，随着时间的推移，持续开源优秀的模型⾮常重要，我
们打算继续这样做。 ”
他还表⽰，开放模型对于美国在 AI 竞争中保持优势⾄关重要，并称： “ 世界上⼤多数 AI 都将运⾏
在开放模型上。它们应该是美国的。 ”
这⼀表态也将 Muse Spark Open 放在全球竞争的背景下。阿⾥巴巴、 DeepSeek 等中国企业已在
开放模型领域取得⼴泛关注，开发者能够根据⾃⾝需求调整模型，并将其部署到不同的硬件和
应⽤中。 Meta 希望通过开放权重提供具有竞争⼒的美国替代⽅案，巩固其在全球开发者⽣态中
的影响⼒。
但开放策略也伴随⻛险。模型权重⼀旦⼴泛分发，开发者可以在⼀定程度上⾃⾏调整模型⾏
为，模型提供⽅对最终部署⽅式的控制⼒随之下降。 Meta 表⽰， Muse Spark Open 经过额外安
全训练，并按照公司的安全评估框架进⾏测试；不过，开放模型的安全边界仍取决于后续使⽤
者如何部署和修改模型。
因此， Meta 并未完全回到此前全⾯开放的路线，⽽是采取分层策略：开放权重模型⽤于扩⼤开

发者覆盖⾯，闭源前沿模型则保留技术和商业优势。
免费模型也要变现， Meta 瞄准云端算⼒市场
Muse Spark Open 的另⼀层意义，在于 Meta 希望通过开放模型为⾃⾝云业务和 AI 服务创造需
求。
彭博社报道称， Meta 正在筹建⾃⼰的云业务，计划向客⼾销售 AI 模型访问权限和计算基础设
施。 Alexandr Wang 认为，免费开放 Muse Spark Open 不会削弱这⼀业务，反⽽可以成为吸引
开发者的⼊⼝：⽤⼾先在⾃⼰的设备或基础设施上试⽤模型，随着应⽤规模扩⼤，再为更多算
⼒和⼯具付费。
这⼀模式与 Meta 传统的⼴告业务存在明显区别。开放权重本⾝不直接带来订阅收⼊，但可以扩
⼤模型的使⽤范围，帮助 Meta 建⽴开发者关系，并为后续的云端推理、企业服务和⼯具链创造
商业机会。
与直接向终端⽤⼾销售付费 AI 助⼿相⽐，开放模型的商业回报更依赖⽣态规模和后续服务转化。
开发者是否愿意采⽤ Muse Spark Open ，企业是否愿意为相关云服务付费，都将决定这⼀策略

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Asia Semiconductor Insight
AI put to the test: the new era of chip testing
Time to revisit semiconductor testing as content growth accelerates
Our core thesis is that AI-driven chip complexity is drastically increasing testing content
across wafer, package, and system levels, creating a multi-year growth opportunity that
the market has yet to fully price in. Testing companies in Taiwan have underperformed
TAIEX in the past three months (indexing at -9% vs TAIEX +7%), yet to recover from the

July market deleveraging. Investor hesitation currently stems from 1) fear of test content
growth expectations already priced-in, 2) lingering concerns over shorter test times, and
3) near-term capacity constraints among major suppliers of probe cards, load boards,
socket pins, testers, and handlers, caused by heightened testing demand. Ultimately,
these concerns overshadow the structural increase in test insertions required by AI
accelerators, chiplets, and advanced packaging. As advanced packaging scales, testing

intensity must rise in tandem, driving growth through higher content per device rather
than semiconductor unit growth alone.
Expanding testing opportunities with shift-left for CP and increasing FT content
Chip probing (CP) supply chain should be the key beneficiary of the industry's shift-left
testing trend, as more test insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in
addition to longer CP time. We believe investors underestimate this trend, which should

accelerate with broader adoption of 2.5D/3D packaging, chiplets and larger AI
packages, driving demand for high pin count probe cards, probers, and thermal-control
equipment. We expect strong growth for Hermes Testing (HTSI)  as thermal-
management requirements rise with increasing chip power and complexity, with prober
thermal-control module shipments reaching 3,000/4,100 units in 2027E/28E, up from
1,200 in 2026E . We forecast Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT) 's sales to exceed

consensus by 7%/15% in 2027E/28E, supported by structural AI/HPC testing demand
and rising value-added content. Importantly, shift-left testing does not diminish the role
of final test (FT) and mission-mode testing. Test content and test time should continue to
increase as validation becomes increasingly critical for complex, large-form-factor AI
accelerators. We expect larger package sizes and rising system-level testing
requirements to drive demand for WinWay's high-pin-count sockets, supporting

74%/71% YoY sales growth in 2027E/28E.
Future of testing: co-packaged optics (CPO) will be a major opportunity
CPO testing represents a significant new market opportunity, though we expect the
ecosystem to remain fragmented rather than consolidating around a single winner. As AI
scaling shifts the industry bottleneck from memory to interconnect, CPO adoption is
becoming increasingly important, creating new testing challenges that will require
collaboration across equipment, optics, and test solution providers. Leveraging

ficonTEC's optical alignment expertise and HTSI's probe, testing, and manufacturing
capabilities, we estimate the HTSI  will deliver 40 units of insertion 2 equipment and 70
units of insertion 3 testing equipment in 2027 , with further upside in 2028 as CPO
deployment expands from scale-out towards scale-up.
Initiate Hermes Testing, Chunghwa Precision Test, WinWay with Buy ratings
We initiate coverage on semiconductor testing solution provider Hermes Testing with

a Buy rating,  NT$8,880 price target based on 31x 2028E PE, for thermal control/CPO
with the most attractive risk/reward in our testing coverage; test interface PCB supplier
CHPT with a Buy rating, NT$5,000 price target based on 31x avg 2027-28E PE, for
CPU/TPU programs and test board content gain; and semiconductor test socket leader
WinWay with a Buy rating, NT$8,900 price target based on 30x avg 2027-28E PE,
for socket content growth and margin recovery. Among our testing coverage, we also

including information on the Quantitative Research Review published by UBS, begin on page 22.

Q: Is chip probing becoming increasingly important in the testing supply chain?
Yes.   As package costs rise, foundries must improve known-good-die (KGD) screening before
packaging, increasing demand for wafer-level testing. This should drive demand for probe cards,

probers and advanced thermal control solutions capable of managing higher power consumption,
wafer warpage and tighter test tolerances. We forecast HTSI/CHPT/WinWay' probe card business to
see a 31%/35%/33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E, as they are positioned to grow strongly given
their smaller scale and robust growth drivers from shift left, CPU, AI accelerators. We forecast HTSI to
ship 1,200/3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2026E/27E/28E, as advanced wafer-level test for

AI accelerators will require thermal control solutions in order to prevent probe card needle burnt.
Q: Will FT and SLT content continue to grow in the coming years?
Yes. Despite concerns that final-test (FT) time could flatten or decline with Nvidia's Rubin GPUs, we
expect overall FT content to continue growing as advanced packaging complexity and chip sizes
increase. We also expect broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT)/mission-mode testing, as AI

accelerators require increasingly stringent validation to ensure optimal system and rack-level
performance. We forecast WinWay's socket business to see a 33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E.
Q: Will CPO testing be a revenue generator in 2027-28 to supply chain players?
Yes. Testing remains a key bottleneck, creating a new equipment opportunity beginning in 2027E.
We expect initial CPO volumes in 2027, followed by broader adoption in 2028. Testing remains one

of the key bottlenecks to commercialization, driving demand for new equipment and test solutions.
We believe early leaders such as ficonTEC (partnership with HTSI) are well positioned to benefit from
the initial ramp, alongside suppliers including Chroma ATE and Hon Precision.
1) We believe upside from agentic AI CPU demand is not reflected in CHPT and WinWay shares. We
estimate CPU-related demand could contribute 20-30% of 2027E sales  for CHPT and WinWay,

respectively, with further upside in 2028E. 2) We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO testing
equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our
industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this
should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands.

Testing is becoming one of the largest incremental beneficiaries of AI-driven semiconductor
complexity. As more test insertions are required across wafer, package and system levels, testing
content per device should increase materially, expanding the industry's addressable market and
supporting multi-year growth across the supply chain.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.

TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.

Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
With the industry increasingly adopting shift-left testing, chip probing (CP) and its supply
chain should be key beneficiaries of a multi-year structural growth trend. This underpins

our above-consensus forecasts for Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), with 2027E/28E
sales 7%/15% above consensus driven by stronger AI/HPC demand, capacity expansion,
and rising value-added content. WinWay should also benefit through its partnership
with Technoprobe, with probe card revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in
2026E/27E following 161%/260% growth in 2024/25.
Hermes Testing (HTSI) should be another major beneficiary as increasing chip power

and complexity drive demand for advanced thermal-management modules used
alongside high-end probing systems. With TEL's leading AI-oriented probers typically
paired with advanced thermal-control solutions, we expect HTSI's thermal-module
shipments to reach 3,000 units in 2027E from 1,200 in 2026E. Combined with testing,
installation and service revenue, this should drive HTSI sales growth of 127%/152% in
2026E/27E.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver

Nvidia remains a key growth driver for both CHPT and WinWay. We expect both
companies to gain share with Rubin, with WinWay's socket share improving from
roughly 50% historically to 60% with Rubin, while CHPT is positioned to supply around
70% of probe card and final test PCB content. Nvidia currently accounts for roughly
50% of WinWay's revenue and 30% of CHPT's.
Beyond Nvidia, we view Google's TPU roadmap as a meaningful incremental
opportunity. Following the successful ramp of TPU v8t with MediaTek as design partner,

CHPT is positioned to supply probe card PCBs, final test load boards, system-level test
boards and burn-in boards. WinWay will supply sockets for both TPU v8t and v9, with
the larger v9 package providing another growth leg. We estimate production of 4mn
TPU v8t units in 2027E and 2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) alongside 2m v8t units
in 2028E.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface in an agentic
world
With agentic AI proliferation, CPU demand is increasing significantly and this directly

correlates to increasing demand for testing consumables. WinWay and CHPT should
both benefit from the strong ramp up of Nvidia's ARM based Vera CPU and Google's
ARM based Axion CPU in 2026E and 2027E. WinWay will be supplying sockets and
probe cards for Vera and Axion, and CHPT will be the probe card and PCB supplier for
both Vera and Axion. WinWay will have the additional upside from AMD's x86 Venice
CPU, and CHPT could capture some share through AMD's equipment vendor

procurement. We estimate CHPT's CPU related sales to be low to mid-teens % of total
sales in 2026E and growing to 20-30% in 2027E  with CHPT's share expansions with
turnkey probe card solutions for Google's Axion. For WinWay, we believe CPU should
account for around 30% of sales in 2026E/27E.

### Page 4

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 4
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
ficonTEC's 25 years of precision optical-test expertise makes it an early leader in CPO test
equipment, particularly in optical alignment, a critical capability for high-volume
manufacturing. Through its partnership with ficonTEC, HTSI is well positioned to
benefit from the initial CPO ramp in 2027E-28E by supporting manufacturing scale-up.

We expect both insertion 2 and insertion 3 testing to be required over the next two
years. Despite the technical challenges of double-sided wafer probing, insertion 2
testing remains important given current optical-engine yields and the need to identify
known-good optical engines before fibre-array-unit (FAU) coupling. This becomes
increasingly critical as switch designs integrate larger numbers of optical engines.
We estimate HTSI will ship 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E , with

further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands and next-generation platforms move
into volume production.
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

### Page 5

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 5
Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector are likely to stand out among competition
with greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry. Below we highlight Hermes
Testing (HTSI), Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), and WinWay's impressive growth
drivers and how this compares with broader industry sales CAGR.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly  be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now have substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damage.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years,  bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.
 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules
should start to ship in 2027E which will drive tool ASP up around 25-50%.
 Demand supported by Advantest expansions. We believe the 3,000 thermal
control modules shipment forecast for 2027E is not far-fetched, as we read-across
from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with
probers with a 1:1 ratio) with increasing capacity up to 5,000 testers within

FY2027/3 and they also announced a 10,000-unit target within CY2028.
Although the testers capacity expansion plan includes testers for final test, we do
see a good portion to be allocated to chip probing given the shift-left testing trend
increasing both testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.
 CPO is another brand new TAM for semiconductor testing.  As insertion 2
and 3 testing equipment demand starts to rise in 2027E with scale-out CPO

adoption, we expect HTSI to ship 40/70 insertion 2 and 3 equipment in 2027E ,
with this number growing in 2028E to 80/160 and higher in future years as CPO
proliferates, and driving 52% 2026-31E test equipment & engineering services
sales CAGR.
 CPO to see content increase. Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment may start to ship in
2027E, with higher throughput and tool ASP .
 Insertion 2 might scale down, but not likely  any time soon.  Insertion 2
equipment shipment might come down in 2029E and beyond, as foundries scale-

up yield for photonic IC (PIC) in order to lessen the time consuming insertion 2
testing burden.

Uniquely positioned as a company with vertical integration capabilities with MEMS
probes, multi-layer PCB capabilities, and automation equipment all-in-house,  CHPT is
set to capture the growing test shift-left trend in the coming years, increasing both
testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.
 CP to scale from a lower base. As a smaller probe card vendor, CHPT can benefit
from the growing demand for AI probe cards, with 2026-31E sales CAGR for

turnkey probe card robust at 41%.
 Also a FT/SLT/burn-in beneficiary. The company will also  see final test, system-
level test, burn-in content increase to play a role in their sales growth from their
IC Test Board sales CAGR of 39% in 2026-31E.
 Capacity expansion supporting growth.  As CHPT is currently capacity
constrained, the robust projected sales growth is enabled by CHPT's rapid capacity
expansion strategy with strong demand for its products, with one production

expansion scheduled for August 2026 and a second in March 2027, doubling and
tripling year-end 2025 capacity, respectively. Its Fab 3 will come online in Q428,
doubling existing capacity when fully ramped.
 MEMS capability to support share gain.  CHPT has MEMS probe capabilities
stemming from its history as a smartphone application processor (AP) probe card
supplier. It is in a good position to capture the growing demand for MEMS probe
cards as AI/HPC are shifting to MEMS probes.

 Probe card content expect to rise.  We also expect probe card content per
device to continue rising as AI accelerators grow in die size, pin count, power
consumption and packaging complexity. This should drive secular growth for both
probe card suppliers and upstream component providers, making chip probing
one of the most attractive areas within the semiconductor testing value chain.

The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test

consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and
next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to
Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with

TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity rising from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm
in 2026E and further expanding to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI
accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal

design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from

HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,
which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.

Below we summarize key technical items of probe cards, sockets, and load boards/PCB
used for semiconductor testing:
Probe cards and chip probing (CP)
Probe cards are used during chip probing (CP), the wafer-level stage of semiconductor

testing that identifies known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. As advanced
packaging technologies such as CoWoS, 3D stacking and hybrid bonding become more
prevalent, CP is becoming increasingly important because a single defective die or HBM
stack can compromise the entire package. This is driving the industry's shift-left testing
strategy, with more testing conducted earlier in the manufacturing process to identify
defects before costly packaging steps.

Probe cards consist of contact elements (probes) mounted on a PCB and are used
together with probers and automated test equipment (ATE). During testing, wafers are
placed on the prober chuck while the probe card makes electrical contact with individual
dies. The tester then performs electrical, parametric, and functional tests to determine
whether each die meets performance requirements. Dies that fail testing are removed
from the production flow prior to dicing and packaging.

Furthermore, probe cards are highly customised products designed for specific chip
architectures and testing platforms. As AI accelerators adopt larger dies, higher pin
counts and more advanced packaging technologies, probe card complexity and content
value continue to increase.

### Page 9

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 9
Figure 12: Chip probing utilise probe
cards to find known-good dies (KGD) and
eliminate faulty dies
Chip/Wafer Probing

The global probe card market remains concentrated among a handful of suppliers:
 FormFactor (FORM.O): World's largest probe card company (including both logic
and memory probe cards) by revenue as of year end 2025. Key supplier for HBM

probe cards.
 Technoprobe (TPRO.MI): World's largest logic probe card company by revenue
as of year end 2025. Key supplier for AI accelerators MEMS probe cards, especially
for GPU.
 MJC (6871.T): Japanese company, mainly specializing in memory probe cards, 3rd
largest probe card company by revenue as of year end 2025.
 MPI (6223.TWO) : Traditionally specializes in cantilever  probe cards, now
expanding its MEMS probe capacity. 4th largest probe card company by revenue as

of year end 2025. A probe card supplier of AI ASICs.
 JEM (6855.T): Mainly specialize in memory probe cards, 5th largest probe card
company by revenue as of year end 2025.
 WinWay (6515.TWO): One of the fastest-growing logic probe card suppliers.
Following its collaboration with Technoprobe, WinWay rose from the 15th-largest
probe card supplier in 2024 to the fifth-largest logic probe card supplier in 2025.
Probe card revenue increased from 11% of sales in 2024 to 29% in 2025.

 Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (6510.TW) : A key supplier for smartphone
application processor (AP) probe cards, and has been migrating to HPC related
applications in recent years. Although its share within the probe card market is
minor, it is a major supplier for probe card PCB, especially for high end probe cards
with advanced PCB that has high layer counts (>40 layers, and over 80 layers for AI
applications). Its key competitive advantage is its vertical integration as it

manufacturers the MEMS probe and PCB in-house.

Probe cards generally employ either vertical or cantilever architectures. Vertical probe
cards use probes arranged perpendicular to the substrate, enabling higher pin density

### Page 10

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 10
and finer pitch capability. They have become the standard for AI, HPC and other
advanced semiconductor applications. Cantilever probe cards use angled probes
extending from the PCB and are typically used in analog, driver IC and selected memory
applications where pin-count and pitch requirements are less demanding. The latest
generation of AI accelerators increasingly relies on MEMS (Micro-Electro-Mechanical

Systems) based vertical probe cards, which support ultra-fine pitches, higher pin counts
and improved probing precision. Technoprobe currently leads this segment with pitch
capability reaching approximately 40 micrometres.
For leading-edge AI applications, probe cards must increasingly support:
1. Higher current-carrying capability (CCC).
2. Stable contact resistance.
3. Lower parasitic inductance for signal integrity.
4. Greater durability under varying thermal conditions.

For CHPT, with their MEMS probe capabilities stemming from their smartphone
application processor probe card supply, they are in a good position to capture the
growing demand for MEMS probe cards as AI/HPC are shifting to MEMS probes. We also
expect probe card content per device to continue rising as AI accelerators grow in die
size, pin count, power consumption and packaging complexity. This should drive secular
growth for both probe card suppliers and upstream component providers, making chip

probing one of the most attractive areas within the semiconductor testing value chain.

Sockets
Semiconductor test sockets are electro-mechanical interfaces that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are

placed on to load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets enable
the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged chips),
with tester signals fanning out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 16: Socket illustration
Source: UBS

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 11
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with
high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT

and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.
Compared with the probe card market, the socket industry is more fragmented. Key
suppliers include:
 WinWay (6515.TW) : A major share gainer in AI/HPC sockets, with strong
exposure to Nvidia and AMD. The company specializes in high-performance
coaxial sockets and is developing its next-generation HyperSocket platform. It is
also expanding into probe cards through its partnership with Technoprobe.

 Leeno (058470.KQ) : Vertically integrated supplier focused on smartphone AP
and memory sockets.
 ISC (095340.KQ): Leading memory socket supplier with elastomer expertise.
 Smiths Interconnect (Acquired by Molex) : A key player in AI/HPC socket
industry, with its DaVinci coaxial sockets able to support high frequency
connectivity testing.
 Yamaichi (6941.T): Leading Japanese socket supplier with strong automotive
exposure.
 Yokowo (6800.T): Major provider of contact probes and socket pins.

 Cohu (COHU.O) : Broad test-solutions provider spanning ATE, handlers and
sockets, with greater auto/industrial/consumer  exposure.

AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial

sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.
Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring

probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth
and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets.
The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin counts and faster data-transfer

speeds should continue to increase socket content per device. We expect this trend to
support long-term growth in the socket market, with technology leaders such as
WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-generation solutions including

HyperSocket.

Load Board/Interface board
Load boards are specialised printed circuit boards (PCBs) that connect the tester to the
device under test (DUT) during final test (FT) and system-level test (SLT). Acting as the
electrical interface between the packaged chip and automated test equipment (ATE),

load boards are responsible for power delivery and high-speed signal transmission
throughout the testing process. As AI and HPC devices become increasingly complex,
load board requirements continue to rise. Multilayer construction is crucial as modern
load boards often require more than 50 layers for smartphone application processors
and over 80 layers for leading-edge AI accelerators to support higher pin counts, dense
signal routing and greater power delivery demands. At the same time, impedance

control is an important factor as maintaining signal integrity is becoming increasingly
important as interconnect bandwidth rises and high-speed SerDes blocks become more
prevalent. These trends are driving greater technology content and value per load board.
We expect load board content to increase alongside AI chip complexity, larger package
sizes and higher interface speeds, benefiting suppliers with advanced multilayer PCB
capabilities such as CHPT.

Figure 22: CHPT's AP load board
Source: Company data (CHPT)
Figure 23: CHPT's AI load board
Source: Company data (CHPT)
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. This

### Page 13

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 13
underpins our above-consensus forecasts for Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), with
2027E/28E sales 7%/15% above consensus driven by structural AI/HPC probe card and
test consumables demand, capacity expansion, and increasing test interface content as
test insertions and test time increase. WinWay should also benefit through its growing
probe card business alongside partner Technoprobe, with probe card sales expected to

grow 94%/72% YoY in 2026E/27E following 161%/260% growth in 2024/25.
Figure 24: CP is set to grow substantially with  shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step
